## 1. What this report holds and how to read it

A collection: the member words, the Strong's codes behind them, and what the study has recorded for the occurrences of those words, from the base data through the observations and the claims into the narrative. Sections 2 and 3 say what the data is; 4 and 5 say what the study has recorded; 6 lists the phrases; 7 lists the faults found in the stored rows. Verses are in Scripture order (book in cfg_book_order, chapter, verse). The detail that does not fit a reading (every Strong's entry in full, every observation and node, the change history, the narrative text) is in the workbook, one sheet for each listing; the sheet is named where it is cited.

### 3.1 H2451 חׇכְמָה chokh.mah "wisdom": 143 own occurrences in 135 verses

- **Senses (first level of the meaning tree of the lemma H2451):** wisdom.
- **Related terms (6):** H2449 cha.kham "be wise"; H2450 cha.kham "wise"; H2452 chokh.mah "wisdom"; H2453G chakh.mo.ni "Hachmonite"; H2453H chakh.mo.ni "Hachmoni"; H8461 tach.ke.mo.ni "Tahchemonite".
- **Clusters (cluster_strong):** M16 Wisdom & Folly (heuristic).
- **Counts, three measures as stored:** STEP token count 153; verses STEP attests 145; verse_lexical rows the parse found 153.

- **Carried by the member words:** wisdom (143).
- **Engagement of these occurrences:** none recorded 143.

### 3.2 H0995 בִּין bin "to understand": 125 own occurrences in 122 verses

- **Meaning as stored:** Hebrew; native word בִּין, transliteration bin; STEP gloss "to understand"; sense head "to understand" (the code is its own lemma).
- **Senses (first level of the meaning tree of the lemma H0995):** to discern, understand, consider; (TWOT) prudent, regard.
- **Related terms (24):** H0946 bu.nah "Bunah"; H0996G ba.yin "between"; H0996H ba.yin "between: among"; H0998 bi.nah "understanding"; H1002 bi.rah "palace"; H1003 bi.ra.nit "fortress"; more in the workbook.
- **Clusters (cluster_strong):** M15 Knowing & Understanding (None).
- **Counts, three measures as stored:** STEP token count 170; verses STEP attests 162; verse_lexical rows the parse found 170.

- **Carried by the member words:** understand (45), understanding (30), consider (9), discern (7), understands (7), understood (6), discerning (5), intelligent (3), perceive (3), prudent (2), can discern (1), can feel (1), considering (1), dealt wisely (1), discerned (1), discerning men (1), insight (1), perceives (1).
- **Engagement of these occurrences:** none recorded 125.

- **Meaning as stored:** Hebrew; native word חָכָם, transliteration cha.kham; STEP gloss "wise"; sense head "wise" (the code is its own lemma).

## 4. What the study has recorded for these occurrences

Of 1106 occurrences, 0 have an engagement observation and 1106 have none recorded. There are 365 live observations on them (0 engagement, 332 of other recorded stages, 33 of the retired catalogue stages) and 18 withdrawn.

### 4.1 Engagement: what each word does for the inner being (0 observations)

An engagement observation states, for the surface in a verse, whether it voices an inner-being operation (use, face) or is silent. Each is one statement applied to every occurrence it covers.

_no engagement observation covers these occurrences_

The verse before sets up the pair: "the patient in spirit is better than the proud in spirit" (7:8); Its duration is limited to a day: "Be angry and do not sin; do not let the sun go down on your anger, and give no opportunity to the devil" (Eph 4:26–27). - **Observation 14196** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): Part 2 verses: what they add. The four strands hold, and each gains an instance: Strand (a), a demand no one can meet, with the angry one now the demander. "There is not a man on earth who can meet the king's demand … no one can show it to the king except the gods, whose dwelling is not with flesh" (Dan 2:10–11). Then "Because of this the king was angry and very furious, and commanded that all the wise men of Babylon be destroyed" (2:12). This is the mirror of Gen 30:2. - **Observation 14252** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): What it does: Balaam cannot pronounce it: "How can I curse whom God has not cursed? How can I denounce whom the Lord has not denounced?" (Num 23:8). - **Observation 15334** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): The heart is where it happens: "your heart was penitent" (2Ki 22:19); "your heart was tender" (2Ch 34:27); "if then their uncircumcised heart is humbled" (Lev 26:41); "you … have not humbled your heart" (Dan 5:22); "Hezekiah humbled himself for the pride of his heart" (2Ch 32:26). The verse before says "his heart was proud" (32:25). Here humbling is the heart's turn back from its own pride: the same heart, in the other direction; "from the first day that you set your heart to understand and humbled yourself before your God" (Dan 10:12). - **Observation 16932** (prior-capture, tag statement, status draft): What the pattern shows [Claude reading]: The merry heart is a threshold state. In every narrative it comes after eating and drinking and immediately before a decisive event, which others usually initiate. The good heart lowers guard. What enters at that moment decides the outcome: murder (Amnon); exposure and anger (Vashti, Ahasuerus); mockery turned to catastrophe (Samson); outrage (Gibeah); approach and redemption (Ruth); a dead heart the next morning (Nabal); God's claim on the soul (the rich fool); The heart's condition swings overnight. Nabal: merry heart → dead heart (1Sa 25:36–37). Ahasuerus: merry heart → burning anger (Est 1:10–12). The heart's state is not stable; wine sets it high ... (cut; the whole text is in the workbook)

(cut; the whole text is in the workbook)

  - applies to 1 occurrence(s): Pro 31:16 (considers)

## 5. Claims and the narrative

61 claims rest on these occurrences: 4 on an observation of these words and 57 reached only because one of their sources is a verse where a member word stands. The second group is about its verse, not about the collection; it is kept so that nothing stored is hidden, and labelled.

### 5.1 Claims resting on an observation of these words (4)

- **C-359** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): Looking ahead weighs ability and cost before acting, plans under uncertainty, carries a person's word, can part companions or set one apart from a council's decision, and can plan another's good.
  - beyond the text: reads the voyage of Acts 27 as the fullest picture of planning without knowing
  - rests on: observation 12518 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Pro 31:16)
  - told in: chapter 10.7 Choosing and setting direction, Planning
- **C-361** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): God also thinks and plans: his plan stands where human plans cross it, he takes thought for the one in need, and he speaks in his heart.
  - beyond the text: sets God's thinking beside human thinking as the same words with a different end
  - rests on: observation 12450 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Joh 11:50)
  - told in: chapter 11 Other sides of the inner being, 8. God has a heart and a soul; chapter 10.4 Thinking, Small, passing, and wholly seen; chapter 13.1 Divine, Purposing
- **C-365** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): Devising is also design and skill, given by God; its direction makes it good or evil, toward God's house, a king's strength or one's own ease.
  - beyond the text: reads design as one skill whose use changes its character
  - rests on: observation 12414 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Pro 2:11)
  - told in: chapter 10.4 Thinking, What thinking does
- **C-366** (narrative, verdict New, status current, in narrative 1): Thoughts often arise unbidden, at night, and what arises is shaped from outside: by God's gift of skill, by instruction, or by blinding.
  - beyond the text: reads the arising thought as not chosen, from where the verses place it
  - rests on: observation 12411 (strand-reading key-statement, live; Pro 1:4, Pro 8:12)
  - told in: chapter 10.4 Thinking, Where thoughts come from

## 7. Stored-data faults

Blatant errors and omissions in the stored rows, each found by the rule stated. A fault is shown with the rows that identify it; the first 25 of a long list are shown and every row is in the workbook sheet `Faults`.

| rule | what it looks for | found |
|---|---|---|
| link-to-missing-or-deleted-term | live collection_link of this collection whose term_id has no live surface_term row | 0 |
| collection-merge-inconsistent | collection.status and collection.merged_into_id disagree, or the target is not live | 0 |
| member-surface-with-no-occurrence | member term of origin 'surface' that no live verse_lexical row carries | 0 |
| occurrence-in-deleted-verse | occurrence whose verse row is soft deleted | 0 |
| live-node-on-deleted-observation | ib_node with deleted=0 whose observation is deleted or missing (the node still names a verse and word the observation no longer covers) | 0 |
| claim-source-on-deleted-observation | narrative_claim_source naming an observation that is deleted or missing | 0 |
| claim-in-narrative-with-no-current-telling | narrative_claim.in_narrative=1 and no current narrative_claim_telling | 0 |
| live-node-with-no-verse-lexical-id | live ib_node in a verse of the report with a member surface but verse_lexical_id NULL (stored, tied to no occurrence) | 0 |

## 8. Sources

Occurrences: live verse_lexical rows, with the verse text from table `verse`; Strong's data from `strong`, `strong_sense`, `strong_meaning_tree`, `strong_related`, `cluster_strong`; observations and nodes from `ib_observation` and `ib_node` (an observation is withdrawn when deleted = 1); claims from `narrative_claim_source` (by observation_id or verse_id), `narrative_claim`, `narrative_claim_telling` and `narrative_section`. A table or column was used only after its recorded meaning in `cfg_table` and `cfg_column` was read (rule B:123).
